    96	
    97	def aligned_group_capacity(rows: int, groups: int, alignment: int = _GROUP_ALIGNMENT) -> int:
    98	    """Rows a buffer needs so `groups` groups holding `rows` payload rows partition it aligned.
    99	
   100	    Rounding each group's extent up to ``alignment`` can cost at most one extra alignment per
   101	    group beyond rounding the total up once, and that worst case is never reached by every group
   102	    at once, so ``groups - 1`` alignments of slack is both sufficient and tight. The result is
   103	    itself a multiple of ``alignment``, which is what lets the last group absorb the leftover and
   104	    still divide it.
   105	
   106	    ``alignment == 1`` is the unaligned layout, where extents are the payload counts and no slack
   107	    is needed at all.
   108	    """
   109	    if alignment < 1:
   110	        raise ValueError(f"alignment must be positive, got {alignment}")
   111	    if alignment == 1:
   112	        return rows
   113	    return -(-rows // alignment) * alignment + alignment * (groups - 1)
   114	
   115	
   116	def full_partition_offsets(rows: int, group_sizes: jax.Array) -> jax.Array:
   117	    """Per-group end rows that partition all ``rows``, giving the last group the leftover.
   118	
   119	    The kernel's contract is not only that every extent divides `_GROUP_ALIGNMENT`; the final
   120	    offset must also equal the operand's physical row count, since the kernel walks its groups
   121	    across one TMA descriptor over the whole buffer. Charging the residual rows to the last group
   122	    satisfies the second half. They contribute nothing to its product as long as they are zero in
   123	    one of the two operands, which is what every caller here guarantees.
   124	    """
   125	    group_sizes = jnp.asarray(group_sizes, dtype=jnp.int32)
   126	    residual = rows - jnp.sum(group_sizes, dtype=jnp.int32)
   127	    return jnp.cumsum(group_sizes.at[-1].add(residual), dtype=jnp.int32)
   128	
   129	
   130	def pad_grouped_rows(values: jax.Array, group_sizes: jax.Array) -> tuple[jax.Array, jax.Array]:
   131	    """Insert zero rows so each contiguous expert group ends on a `_GROUP_ALIGNMENT` boundary."""
   132	    if values.ndim != 2:
   133	        raise ValueError(f"values must be rank 2, got shape={values.shape}")
   134	    if values.shape[0] == 0:
   135	        raise ValueError("values must contain at least one physical row")
   136	    if group_sizes.ndim != 1 or group_sizes.shape[0] == 0:
   137	        raise ValueError(f"group_sizes must be a nonempty vector, got shape={group_sizes.shape}")
   138	
   139	    group_sizes = group_sizes.astype(jnp.int32)
   140	    groups = group_sizes.shape[0]
   141	
   142	    # The kernel's contract has two halves: every group extent divides `_GROUP_ALIGNMENT`, AND the
   143	    # final offset equals the buffer's physical row count. Rounding each extent up satisfies only
   144	    # the first and leaves a ragged tail outside the last group, so give the last group every
   145	    # remaining row instead. Those extra rows are zero and contribute nothing to its product.
   146	    #
   147	    # The physical size is static (it must not depend on runtime routing): rounding the capacity up
   148	    # and adding one alignment per preceding group is always enough for the rounded extents.
   149	    padded_capacity = aligned_group_capacity(values.shape[0], groups)
   150	
   151	    aligned_sizes = ((group_sizes + _GROUP_ALIGNMENT - 1) // _GROUP_ALIGNMENT) * _GROUP_ALIGNMENT
   152	    active_offsets = jnp.cumsum(group_sizes, dtype=jnp.int32)
   153	    padded_offsets = full_partition_offsets(padded_capacity, aligned_sizes)
   154	    active_starts = jnp.concatenate([jnp.zeros((1,), jnp.int32), active_offsets[:-1]])
   155	    padded_starts = jnp.concatenate([jnp.zeros((1,), jnp.int32), padded_offsets[:-1]])
   156	
   157	    padded_rows = jnp.arange(padded_capacity, dtype=jnp.int32)
   158	    expert_ids = jnp.sum(padded_rows[:, None] >= padded_offsets[None, :], axis=1, dtype=jnp.int32)
   159	    safe_expert_ids = jnp.minimum(expert_ids, group_sizes.shape[0] - 1)
   160	    rows_within_group = padded_rows - padded_starts[safe_expert_ids]
   161	    source_rows = active_starts[safe_expert_ids] + rows_within_group
   162	    valid = (expert_ids < group_sizes.shape[0]) & (rows_within_group < group_sizes[safe_expert_ids])
   163	    source_rows = jnp.clip(source_rows, 0, values.shape[0] - 1)
   164	    padded = jnp.where(valid[:, None], values[source_rows], jnp.zeros((), dtype=values.dtype))
   165	    return padded, padded_offsets
   166	
   167	
   168	def cudnn_grouped_wgrad(lhs: jax.Array, rhs: jax.Array, group_sizes: jax.Array) -> jax.Array:
   169	    """Compute per-expert ``lhs.T @ rhs`` for contiguous ragged row groups.
   170	
   171	    Both operands are copied into the kernel's aligned layout first, which is what
   172	    `pad_grouped_rows` costs. A caller whose buffers already carry that layout should use
   173	    `cudnn_grouped_wgrad_prealigned`.
   174	    """
   175	    _assert_group_alignment_matches_kernel()
   176	    lhs_padded, padded_offsets = pad_grouped_rows(lhs, group_sizes)
   177	    rhs_padded, _ = pad_grouped_rows(rhs, group_sizes)
   178	    return _grouped_wgrad(lhs_padded, rhs_padded, padded_offsets, expert_count=group_sizes.shape[0])
   179	
   180	
   181	def cudnn_grouped_wgrad_prealigned(lhs: jax.Array, rhs: jax.Array, group_sizes: jax.Array) -> jax.Array:
   182	    """`cudnn_grouped_wgrad` for operands already in the kernel's aligned layout.
   183	
   184	    The caller guarantees that group ``i`` occupies rows
   185	    ``[sum(group_sizes[:i]), sum(group_sizes[:i+1]))`` of both operands, that every entry of
   186	    ``group_sizes`` is a multiple of `_GROUP_ALIGNMENT` (so every group starts on an aligned
   187	    row), and that any row inside a group that carries no data is zero in at least one operand.
   188	
   189	    Any rows past the last group are folded into it by `full_partition_offsets`, because the
   190	    kernel requires the final offset to equal the operand's row count -- it addresses every group
   191	    through one descriptor over the whole buffer, so a group that ends short reads on into
   192	    whatever follows. The buffer's own slack is what gets folded in, and it is zero.
   193	
   194	    Under that contract this is `cudnn_grouped_wgrad` with the pad pass removed: `pad_grouped_rows`
   195	    applied to such a layout reproduces it row for row and returns the same offsets, so the kernel
   196	    sees byte-identical operands and offsets either way.
   197	    """
   198	    _assert_group_alignment_matches_kernel()
   199	    offsets = full_partition_offsets(lhs.shape[0], group_sizes)
   200	    return _grouped_wgrad(lhs, rhs, offsets, expert_count=group_sizes.shape[0])
   201	
   202	
   203	def _grouped_wgrad(lhs: jax.Array, rhs: jax.Array, offsets: jax.Array, *, expert_count: int) -> jax.Array:
   204	    """Call the kernel over row groups delimited by ``offsets``, the per-group end rows."""
   205	    if lhs.ndim != 2 or rhs.ndim != 2:
   206	        raise ValueError(f"lhs and rhs must be rank 2, got lhs={lhs.shape}, rhs={rhs.shape}")
   207	    if lhs.shape[0] != rhs.shape[0]:
   208	        raise ValueError(f"lhs and rhs row counts must match, got lhs={lhs.shape}, rhs={rhs.shape}")
   209	    if lhs.dtype != jnp.bfloat16 or rhs.dtype != jnp.bfloat16:
   210	        raise ValueError(f"cuDNN grouped Wgrad requires BF16 inputs, got lhs={lhs.dtype}, rhs={rhs.dtype}")
   211	    if lhs.shape[1] % _FEATURE_ALIGNMENT != 0 or rhs.shape[1] % _FEATURE_ALIGNMENT != 0:
   212	        raise ValueError(
   213	            f"cuDNN grouped Wgrad feature dimensions must divide {_FEATURE_ALIGNMENT}, "
   214	            f"got lhs={lhs.shape}, rhs={rhs.shape}"
   215	        )
   216	
   217	    modules = _cudnn_modules()
   218	    max_active_clusters = modules.cutlass.utils.HardwareInfo().get_max_active_clusters(
   219	        _CLUSTER_SHAPE_MN[0] * _CLUSTER_SHAPE_MN[1]
   220	    )
   221	    launcher = _build_launcher(
   222	        modules,
   223	        expert_count=expert_count,
   224	        max_active_clusters=max_active_clusters,
   225	    )
   226	    tensor_spec = modules.cutlass_jax.TensorSpec
   227	    call = cutlass_call(
   228	        launcher,
   229	        output_shape_dtype=(
   230	            jax.ShapeDtypeStruct((expert_count, lhs.shape[1], rhs.shape[1]), lhs.dtype),
   231	            jax.ShapeDtypeStruct((1,), jnp.uint8),
   232	        ),
   233	        input_spec=(
   234	            tensor_spec(mode=(1, 0), divisibility=(1, _FEATURE_ALIGNMENT), static=False),
   235	            tensor_spec(divisibility=(1, _FEATURE_ALIGNMENT), static=False),
   236	            tensor_spec(static=False),
   237	        ),
   238	        output_spec=(
   239	            tensor_spec(divisibility=(1, 1, _FEATURE_ALIGNMENT), static=False),
   240	            tensor_spec(static=False),
   241	        ),
   242	        use_static_tensors=False,
   243	    )
   244	    output, _workspace = call(lhs, rhs, offsets)
   245	    return output
   120	        clipped_by_receiver.append(jnp.stack(accepted_rows, axis=0))
   121	
   122	    return jnp.concatenate(clipped_by_receiver, axis=1)
   123	
   124	
   125	def _align_up_rows(values: Int[Array, "..."], alignment: int) -> Int[Array, "..."]:
   126	    """Round each row count up to a multiple of ``alignment``.
   127	
   128	    ``alignment == 1`` returns the input array itself, so a caller that leaves alignment off
   129	    emits no arithmetic at all and traces exactly the graph it traced before this existed.
   130	    """
   131	    if alignment < 1:
   132	        raise ValueError(f"alignment must be positive, got {alignment}")
   133	    if alignment == 1:
   134	        return values
   135	    return ((values + alignment - 1) // alignment) * alignment
   136	
   137	
   138	class ExpertA2aParams(NamedTuple):
   139	    """Offset/size vectors for one direction of an expert-granular ``ragged_all_to_all``."""
   140	
   141	    input_offsets: Int[Array, "U"]
   142	    send_sizes: Int[Array, "U"]
   143	    output_offsets: Int[Array, "U"]
   144	    recv_sizes: Int[Array, "U"]
   145	
   146	
   147	def _expert_granular_a2a_params(
   148	    all_group_sizes: Int[Array, "S E"],
   149	    clipped_group_sizes: Int[Array, "S E"],
   150	    shard_id: Int[Array, ""],
   151	    *,
   152	    local_expert_size: int,
   153	    receiver_alignment: int = 1,
   154	) -> tuple[ExpertA2aParams, ExpertA2aParams]:
   155	    """Build dispatch and return ``ragged_all_to_all`` parameters at (peer, expert) granularity.
   156	
   157	    One update per (destination shard, local expert). Sender reads each global-expert
   158	    group at its *unclipped* offset with its *clipped* size, so accepted rows need no
   159	    compaction: they are the prefix of each group. Receiver offsets pack arriving rows
   160	    expert-major (sender-major within each expert), so the received buffer needs no local
   161	    permute before the grouped MLP. The return direction is the exact mirror: it reads the
   162	    expert-major receiver buffer and writes valid prefixes back to the sender's unclipped
   163	    positions, leaving dropped rows at the output operand's values.
   164	
   165	    ``receiver_alignment`` rounds each receiver group's *extent* up to a multiple of that many
   166	    rows, which starts every group on an aligned row and leaves up to ``alignment - 1`` unwritten
   167	    rows between groups. It changes only where the receiver puts each group: senders read the
   168	    same rows and send the same sizes, the accepted set is untouched, and the return direction
   169	    reads the same valid prefixes back out. The receiver buffer must then hold
   170	    ``capacity + (alignment - 1) * groups`` rows, since the per-group slack is charged to the
   171	    buffer rather than to the capacity that decides drops.
   172	    """
   173	    num_shards = all_group_sizes.shape[0]
   174	
   175	    # [src, dest, e]: rows sender `src` contributes to `dest`'s local expert `e`.
   176	    clipped = clipped_group_sizes.reshape(num_shards, num_shards, local_expert_size)
   177	
   178	    # Sender side: unclipped group starts in this shard's expert-sorted buffer.
   179	    unclipped_starts = jnp.cumsum(all_group_sizes, axis=1) - all_group_sizes
   180	    my_send = clipped[shard_id]  # [dest, e]
   181	    dispatch_input_offsets = unclipped_starts[shard_id].reshape(num_shards, local_expert_size)
   182	
   183	    # Receiver side: expert-major segment starts on each destination, sender-major within.
   184	    dest_totals = jnp.sum(clipped, axis=0)  # [dest, e]
   185	    group_extents = _align_up_rows(dest_totals, receiver_alignment)  # [dest, e]
   186	    expert_starts = jnp.cumsum(group_extents, axis=1) - group_extents
   187	    senders_before_me = (jnp.cumsum(clipped, axis=0) - clipped)[shard_id]  # [dest, e]
   188	    dispatch_output_offsets = expert_starts + senders_before_me
   189	
   190	    # What each source sends this shard, source-major -- also the return direction's sends.
   191	    inbound = clipped[:, shard_id, :]  # [src, e]
   192	
   193	    dispatch = ExpertA2aParams(
   194	        dispatch_input_offsets.reshape(-1),
   195	        my_send.reshape(-1),
   196	        dispatch_output_offsets.reshape(-1),
   197	        inbound.reshape(-1),
   198	    )
   199	
   200	    # Return: read this shard's expert-major receiver buffer, write back to each original
   201	    # sender's unclipped sorted positions for the experts this shard owns.
   202	    my_expert_starts = expert_starts[shard_id]  # [e]
   203	    senders_before = jnp.cumsum(inbound, axis=0) - inbound  # [src, e]
   204	    return_input_offsets = my_expert_starts[None, :] + senders_before
   205	    my_global_experts = jnp.arange(local_expert_size, dtype=jnp.int32) + shard_id * local_expert_size
   206	    return_output_offsets = unclipped_starts[:, my_global_experts]  # [src, e]
   207	
   208	    ret = ExpertA2aParams(
   209	        return_input_offsets.reshape(-1),
   210	        inbound.reshape(-1),
   211	        return_output_offsets.reshape(-1),
   212	        my_send.reshape(-1),
   213	    )
   214	    return dispatch, ret
   145	    grad_vs_dense_dropped: GradDiff
   146	    dropped_skewed: int
   147	    dropped_skewed_expected: int
   148	
   149	
   150	class RuntimeRow(BaseModel):
   151	    """What the run actually exercised, so a green verdict names the code it covers.
   152	
   153	    The expert-MLP kernels are chosen at trace time from the device's compute capability and the
   154	    installed packages, and the transport kernel from XLA flags the runtime may not recognize.
   155	    Both are environment-dependent, so recording them is the difference between "the ragged EP
   156	    path is correct" and "some ragged EP path was correct somewhere".
   157	    """
   158	
   159	    jax_version: str
   160	    device_kind: str
   161	    quack_grouped_gemm_available: bool
   162	    expert_mlp_silu: str
   163	    expert_mlp_gelu: str
   164	    xla_flags: str
   165	    receiver_alignment: int | None
   166	
   167	
   168	def _runtime_row(receiver_alignment: int | None) -> RuntimeRow:
   169	    alignment = _resolve_receiver_alignment(receiver_alignment)
   170	    return RuntimeRow(
   171	        jax_version=jax.__version__,
   172	        device_kind=jax.devices()[0].device_kind,
   173	        quack_grouped_gemm_available=_quack_grouped_gemm_available(),
   174	        expert_mlp_silu=_select_expert_mlp(jax.nn.silu, receiver_alignment=alignment).__name__,
   175	        expert_mlp_gelu=_select_expert_mlp(jax.nn.gelu, receiver_alignment=alignment).__name__,
   176	        xla_flags=os.environ.get("XLA_FLAGS", ""),
   177	        receiver_alignment=receiver_alignment,
   178	    )
   179	
   180	
   181	class RaggedEpResult(Artifact):
   182	    """Per-seed deviations for the ragged-EP ground-truth check."""

   185	@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)
   675	def test_an_expert_chunk_count_that_does_not_divide_the_local_bank_is_rejected_in_the_backend():
   676	    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="must divide the local expert count=4"):
   677	        _lower_hero_loss_grad(_remat_probe_config(ragged_expert_chunks=3), debug_info=False)
   678	
   679	
   680	def test_the_receiver_alignment_is_inert_unless_it_is_set():
   681	    """Leaving the alignment off must not perturb the lowered step by a single byte.
   682	
   683	    The aligned layout is an arm, not a migration: it is meant to be compared against the runs
   684	    that came before it, so the default has to lower to the module it lowered to before the
   685	    option existed. The treatment then shows up only as the transport buffer's row count --
   686	    two chunk experts over a 148-row chunk capacity become `align_up(148, 256) + 256 = 512`
   687	    rows, the same size `pad_grouped_rows` would have allocated for its copy.
   688	    """
   689	    default_hlo = _lower_hero_loss_grad(_remat_probe_config(), debug_info=False)
   690	    unset_hlo = _lower_hero_loss_grad(_remat_probe_config(ragged_receiver_alignment=None), debug_info=False)
   691	    aligned_hlo = _lower_hero_loss_grad(
   692	        _remat_probe_config(ragged_receiver_alignment=ragged_backend.RAGGED_CUDNN_RECEIVER_ALIGNMENT),
   693	        debug_info=False,
   694	    )
   695	
   696	    assert unset_hlo == default_hlo
   697	    assert default_hlo.count("bf16[148,64]{1,0} ragged-all-to-all") == 6
   698	    assert aligned_hlo.count("bf16[512,64]{1,0} ragged-all-to-all") == 6
   699	    # Only the layout moves: the same collectives, in the same number, over the same axis.
   700	    assert aligned_hlo.count(" ragged-all-to-all(") == default_hlo.count(" ragged-all-to-all(")
   701	    for op in ("all-gather", "all-to-all", "all-reduce", "reduce-scatter"):
   702	        assert aligned_hlo.count(f" {op}(") == default_hlo.count(f" {op}(")
   703	
   704	
   705	def test_remat_save_names_reject_unknown_and_carry_names():
   706	    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="no call site"):
   707	        _remat_probe_config(remat_save_names=("grug_typo",))
   708	    # The layer carry is the offload target, not a saveable.
   709	    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="no call site"):
   710	        _remat_probe_config(remat_save_names=(model.LAYER_CARRY_REMAT_NAME,))

    92	def _build_expert_mlp_cudnn(grouped_wgrad):
    93	    """``_expert_mlp`` with the two weight-gradient GEMMs on ``grouped_wgrad``.
    94	
    95	    The forward output is masked past the last expert group: the grouped GEMMs write only
    96	    the rows inside ``cu``, and those trailing rows flow on through the unpermute and
    97	    combine, so they have to be zero rather than whatever the buffer held.
    98	
    99	    ``grouped_wgrad`` is which cuDNN entry point the weight gradients take, which is fixed per
   100	    caller: `cudnn_grouped_wgrad` pads the operands into the kernel's aligned layout, and
   101	    `cudnn_grouped_wgrad_prealigned` skips that copy for a caller whose ``group_sizes`` already
   102	    describe an aligned layout. Both compute the same function; the second is the same kernel
   103	    call on the same bytes without the pre-pass.
   104	
   105	    A pre-aligned caller passes ``group_sizes`` that partition the whole buffer, so ``cu`` covers
   106	    every row, nothing here is masked, and no output row is left holding what its buffer held.
   107	    """
   108	
   109	    @jax.custom_vjp
   110	    def _expert_mlp_cudnn(x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, group_sizes, cu):
   111	        _gu, h = quack_gated_grouped_gemm(x_dispatch, w13_il, cu, return_preact=True, **_QUACK_GATED_KW)
   112	        y = quack_grouped_gemm(h, moe_w2, cu, b_major="n", **_QUACK_GROUPED_KW)
   113	        return _zero_inactive_grouped_rows(y, cu)
   114	
   115	    def _expert_mlp_cudnn_fwd(x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, group_sizes, cu):
   116	        gu, h = quack_gated_grouped_gemm(x_dispatch, w13_il, cu, return_preact=True, **_QUACK_GATED_KW)
   117	        y = quack_grouped_gemm(h, moe_w2, cu, b_major="n", **_QUACK_GROUPED_KW)
   118	        return _zero_inactive_grouped_rows(y, cu), (x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, gu, h, group_sizes, cu)
   119	
   120	    def _expert_mlp_cudnn_bwd(res, dy):
   121	        x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, gu, h, group_sizes, cu = res
   122	        # The cotangent's trailing rows are whatever the caller's buffer held; the grouped GEMMs
   123	        # contract every row they are handed, so they have to be cleared here too.
   124	        dy = _zero_inactive_grouped_rows(dy, cu)
   125	        dh = quack_grouped_gemm(dy, moe_w2, cu, b_major="k", **_QUACK_GROUPED_KW)
   126	        dw2 = grouped_wgrad(h, dy, group_sizes)
   127	        d_gu = _swiglu_gate_up_backward(gu, dh)
   128	        dx = quack_grouped_gemm(d_gu, w13_il, cu, b_major="k", **_QUACK_GROUPED_KW)
   129	        dx = _zero_inactive_grouped_rows(dx, cu)
   130	        dw13_il = grouped_wgrad(x_dispatch, d_gu, group_sizes)
   131	        gs_ct = np.zeros(group_sizes.shape, dtype=jax.dtypes.float0)
   132	        cu_ct = np.zeros(cu.shape, dtype=jax.dtypes.float0)
   133	        return dx, dw13_il, dw2, gs_ct, cu_ct
   134	
   135	    _expert_mlp_cudnn.defvjp(_expert_mlp_cudnn_fwd, _expert_mlp_cudnn_bwd)
   136	    return _expert_mlp_cudnn
   137	
   138	
   139	_expert_mlp_cudnn = _build_expert_mlp_cudnn(cudnn_grouped_wgrad)
   140	# For a receiver buffer whose expert groups already start on the kernel's aligned rows, with the
   141	# slack between them zero. The transport builds that layout, so the pad pass is pure copy traffic.
   142	_expert_mlp_cudnn_prealigned = _build_expert_mlp_cudnn(cudnn_grouped_wgrad_prealigned)

   145	def _moe_mlp_local_sonic_cute(
   146	    x: Float[Array, "T H"],
   147	    selected_experts: Int[Array, "T K"],
   148	    combine_weights: Float[Array, "T K"],
   149	    moe_w13: Float[Array, "E H I2"],
   150	    moe_w2: Float[Array, "E I H"],
   450	    count is a measurement knob, so an indivisible one raises instead of quietly running -- and
   451	    reporting -- a different chunk count than the one that was asked for.
   452	    """
   453	    if expert_chunks is None:
   454	        return RAGGED_DEFAULT_EXPERT_CHUNKS if local_experts % RAGGED_DEFAULT_EXPERT_CHUNKS == 0 else 1
   455	    if expert_chunks < 1:
   456	        raise ValueError(f"expert_chunks must be positive, got {expert_chunks}")
   457	    if local_experts % expert_chunks != 0:
   458	        raise ValueError(f"expert_chunks={expert_chunks} must divide the local expert count={local_experts}")
   459	    return expert_chunks
   460	
   461	
   462	def _resolve_receiver_alignment(receiver_alignment: int | None) -> int:
   463	    """Row alignment the receiver buffer's expert groups start on.
   464	
   465	    ``None`` is the unaligned layout: groups are packed back to back and the cuDNN
   466	    weight-gradient wrapper copies both of its operands into an aligned buffer itself. Any other
   467	    value lays the groups out aligned at the source and deletes those copies, which is only
   468	    sound if the value is a multiple of what that kernel requires.
   469	    """
   470	    if receiver_alignment is None:
   471	        return 1
   472	    if receiver_alignment < 1:
   473	        raise ValueError(f"receiver_alignment must be positive, got {receiver_alignment}")
   474	    if receiver_alignment % RAGGED_CUDNN_RECEIVER_ALIGNMENT != 0:
   475	        raise ValueError(
   476	            f"receiver_alignment={receiver_alignment} must be a multiple of "
   477	            f"{RAGGED_CUDNN_RECEIVER_ALIGNMENT}, the cuDNN grouped weight-gradient group alignment"
   478	        )
   479	    return receiver_alignment
   480	
   481	
   482	def _moe_mlp_ep_ragged_a2a_local(
   483	    x_local: Float[Array, "Tlocal H"],
   484	    selected_experts_local: Int[Array, "Tlocal K"],
   485	    combine_weights_local: Float[Array, "Tlocal K"],
   486	    moe_w13_local: Float[Array, "Elocal H I2"],
   487	    moe_w2_local: Float[Array, "Elocal I H"],
   488	    *,
   489	    activation_fn: Callable[[jax.Array], jax.Array],
   490	    num_experts: int,
   491	    capacity_factor: float,
   492	    expert_chunks: int | None = None,
   493	    receiver_alignment: int | None = None,
   494	) -> tuple[Float[Array, "Tlocal H"], CapacityOverflow]:
   495	    local_experts = moe_w13_local.shape[0]
   496	    if num_experts % local_experts != 0:
   497	        raise ValueError(
   498	            f"num_experts={num_experts} must be divisible by local expert count={local_experts} in EP mode"
   499	        )
   500	
   501	    shard_id = jax.lax.axis_index("expert")
   502	    ep_size = num_experts // local_experts
   503	    tokens_per_shard = x_local.shape[0]
   504	    topk = selected_experts_local.shape[1]
   505	    assignments_per_shard = tokens_per_shard * topk
   506	    local_capacity = int(math.ceil(capacity_factor * assignments_per_shard))
   507	    local_capacity = max(local_experts, local_capacity)
   508	
   509	    # Local experts are processed in sequential chunks so only one chunk's buffers are live at a
   510	    # time: capacity splits evenly across chunks, so two chunks halve the [C, H] transport
   511	    # buffers and the expert MLP's [C, I2] gate-up intermediate that sit between them. It also
   512	    # makes drop clipping per-chunk, which is a routing difference, not just a memory one.
   513	    #
   514	    # What chunking does not change is what crosses the forward/backward boundary. The block's
   515	    # remat policy re-runs this function in the backward, so the lowered module carries every
   516	    # ragged all-to-all three times at any chunk count -- primal, backward recompute, transpose,
   517	    # verified in the lowered StableHLO: 12 ops at two chunks, 6 at one. The a2a outputs are
   518	    # transient inside a chunk, not residuals pinned across the block window.
   519	    chunks = _resolve_expert_chunks(expert_chunks, local_experts)
   520	    chunk_experts = local_experts // chunks
   521	    chunk_capacity = max(chunk_experts, int(math.ceil(local_capacity / chunks)))
   522	    hidden_dim = x_local.shape[1]
   523	
   524	    # Aligning the receiver's expert groups costs the chunk a bounded number of unwritten rows.
   525	    # Those rows are buffer, not capacity: the clip below still admits `chunk_capacity` rows, so
   526	    # no assignment is accepted or dropped differently. Sizing the buffer with the padding path's
   527	    # own rule is what makes the two layouts the same size, and therefore comparable row for row.
   528	    alignment = _resolve_receiver_alignment(receiver_alignment)
   529	    receiver_rows = aligned_group_capacity(chunk_capacity, chunk_experts, alignment)
   530	
   531	    with jax.named_scope("dispatch"):
   532	        flat_selected = selected_experts_local.reshape(-1)  # [TK]
   533	        sorted_indices = tree_checkpoint_name(jnp.argsort(flat_selected), RAGGED_SORTED_INDICES_REMAT_NAME)  # [TK]
   534	        group_sizes = tree_checkpoint_name(
   535	            jnp.bincount(flat_selected, length=num_experts).astype(jnp.int32), RAGGED_GROUP_SIZES_REMAT_NAME
   536	        )  # [E]
   537	        sorted_x = _gather_dispatch_rows(x_local, sorted_indices, topk)  # [TK, H]
   538	        all_group_sizes = jax.lax.all_gather(group_sizes, "expert")  # [S, E]
   539	
   540	    expert_mlp = _select_expert_mlp(activation_fn, receiver_alignment=alignment)
   541	    chunk_of_expert = (jnp.arange(num_experts, dtype=jnp.int32) % local_experts) // chunk_experts  # [E]
   542	    # Built per-iteration (not jnp.zeros) so the layer scan does not re-copy a hoisted
   543	    # constant into the in-place return a2a's output slot every step; see _loop_local_zeros.
   544	    # The zeros are load-bearing: rows no peer writes back must read 0 in the combine.
   545	    returned = _loop_local_zeros(assignments_per_shard, hidden_dim, x_local.dtype, group_sizes, site=5)  # [TK, H]
   546	    accepted_local = jnp.zeros((), dtype=jnp.int32)
   547	    for chunk_index in range(chunks):
   548	        with jax.named_scope(f"moe_chunk_{chunk_index}"):
   549	            chunk_all_group_sizes = jnp.where(chunk_of_expert[None, :] == chunk_index, all_group_sizes, 0)  # [S, E]
   550	            clipped_group_sizes = _clip_receiver_group_sizes(
   551	                chunk_all_group_sizes,
   552	                local_expert_size=local_experts,
   553	                receiver_capacity=chunk_capacity,
   554	            )
   555	            # Sender starts come from the full (unmasked) sizes, so each chunk reads its
   556	            # groups' accepted prefixes in place in the shared sorted buffer.
   557	            dispatch_params, return_params = _expert_granular_a2a_params(
   558	                all_group_sizes,
   559	                clipped_group_sizes,
   560	                shard_id,
   561	                local_expert_size=local_experts,
   562	                receiver_alignment=alignment,
   563	            )
   564	            # Serialize the chunks. Without this barrier, the scheduler can start the dispatch
   565	            # of every chunk at the same time. This causes the high memory use that the chunks
   566	            # prevent. A variant that overlaps one transport with the MLP stays within memory.
   567	            # But it does not increase the speed. The transport and the MLP compete for the
   568	            # same SMs.
   569	            chunk_source, _ = jax.lax.optimization_barrier((sorted_x, returned))
   570	            # Accepted rows are the prefix of each unclipped expert group and receiver offsets
   571	            # pack arrivals expert-major, so the received buffer feeds the grouped MLP
   572	            # directly: no sender compaction and no receiver-side permute.
   573	            x_dispatch = _dispatch_ragged_a2a(  # [C, H]
   574	                receiver_rows,
   575	                assignments_per_shard,
   576	                chunk_source,
   577	                *dispatch_params,
   578	            )
   579	            active_all = jnp.sum(  # [Elocal]
   580	                clipped_group_sizes.reshape(ep_size, ep_size, local_experts)[:, shard_id, :], axis=0
   581	            )
   582	            # The group extents the receiver buffer was actually laid out on -- the arrival counts
   583	            # under no alignment, and their rounded-up extents otherwise. The a2a rounds the same
   584	            # per-expert totals the same way, so the two layouts agree by construction.
   585	            active_group_sizes = _align_up_rows(
   586	                active_all[chunk_index * chunk_experts : (chunk_index + 1) * chunk_experts], alignment
   587	            )  # [Echunk]
   588	            total_valid = jnp.sum(active_group_sizes, dtype=jnp.int32)
   589	            # `receiver_rows - total_valid` is nonnegative, and under an aligned layout it is a
   590	            # multiple of the alignment, because `aligned_group_capacity` bounds the rounded
   591	            # extents and is itself a multiple. So the physical sizes stay a legal aligned
   592	            # partition of the whole buffer, which is what the pre-aligned wgrad path needs.
   593	            physical_group_sizes = active_group_sizes.at[-1].add(receiver_rows - total_valid)  # [Echunk]
   594	            out_dispatch = expert_mlp(  # [C, H]
   595	                x_dispatch,
   596	                moe_w13_local[chunk_index * chunk_experts : (chunk_index + 1) * chunk_experts],
   597	                moe_w2_local[chunk_index * chunk_experts : (chunk_index + 1) * chunk_experts],
   598	                physical_group_sizes,
   599	                active_group_sizes,
   600	                activation_fn,
   601	            )
   602	            # The mirror of dispatch: valid prefixes land back at unclipped sorted positions.
   603	            # Chaining every chunk through one output buffer composes the disjoint writes;
   604	            # dropped rows keep the buffer's zeros, so the final gather-sum reads dropped
   605	            # slots as zero contributions with no expansion step.
   606	            returned = _return_ragged_a2a(
   607	                receiver_rows,
   608	                out_dispatch,
   609	                returned,
   610	                *return_params,
   611	            )
   612	            accepted_local = accepted_local + jnp.sum(clipped_group_sizes[shard_id], dtype=jnp.int32)
   613	
   614	    with jax.named_scope("combine"):
   615	        out_local = _unpermute_from_global_expert(
   250	    sconv_kernel: int = 4
   251	    sconv_sites: tuple[str, ...] = ("k", "attn", "mlp")
   252	    attention_implementation: GrugAttentionImplementation | None = None
   253	    moe_implementation: MoeImplementation | None = None
   254	    expert_chunks: int = 1
   255	    ragged_expert_chunks: int | None = None
   256	    """Sequential local-expert chunks in the ragged all-to-all transport, each taking its own
   257	    share of the receiver capacity. None takes the backend default
   258	    (`RAGGED_DEFAULT_EXPERT_CHUNKS`, falling back to one chunk on a local bank it does not
   259	    divide); an explicit count must divide the local bank. Other transports ignore it."""
   260	    ragged_receiver_alignment: int | None = None
   261	    """Row alignment each expert group starts on in the ragged all-to-all receiver buffer. None
   262	    packs the groups back to back and leaves the cuDNN grouped weight-gradient wrapper to copy
   263	    both operands into an aligned buffer itself; a value (a multiple of
   264	    `RAGGED_CUDNN_RECEIVER_ALIGNMENT`) builds that layout in the transport and deletes the copies.
   265	    It moves rows within the buffer, not capacity, so the accepted set is unchanged. Other
   266	    transports ignore it."""
   267	    pooled_transport_capacity_factor: float | None = None
   268	    num_expert_waves: int = 1
   269	    report_capacity_overflow: bool = False
   270	    remat_mode: RematMode = "recompute_all"
   271	    """Per-block gradient checkpointing. "recompute_all" reruns the whole block in
   272	    backward (lowest memory); "save_moe" keeps the tagged MoE dispatch tensors so
   273	    backward skips re-running expert dispatch and its EP collectives; "offload_carry"
   274	    recomputes everything but stages the layer-input residual on pinned host, which
   275	    releases its HBM between forward and backward."""
   276	    remat_save_names: tuple[str, ...] = ()
   277	    """Named intermediates the remat policy keeps on device for the backward, deleting
   278	    their recompute. Names must come from REMAT_SAVEABLE_NAMES; the empty default keeps
   279	    every remat mode's existing policy unchanged. This extends only which named tensors
   280	    are saved -- the offload_carry offload list and the XLA memory-budget flags are
   281	    untouched. Saved values are bit-identical to their recompute; only residency changes."""
   282	    rope: RotaryConfig = dataclasses.field(default_factory=RotaryConfig)
   283	    rope_fused: bool = False
   284	
   285	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
   286	        _ = self.inferred_head_dim
   287	        if self.num_heads % self.stored_kv_heads != 0:
   288	            raise ValueError("num_heads must be divisible by the stored KV-head count")
   289	        if (self.local_kv_heads is None) != (self.global_kv_heads is None):
   290	            raise ValueError("local_kv_heads and global_kv_heads must be set together")
   291	        if self.local_kv_heads is not None and self.global_kv_heads is not None:
   292	            if self.local_kv_heads <= 0 or self.global_kv_heads <= 0:
   293	                raise ValueError("local_kv_heads and global_kv_heads must be positive")
   294	            if self.num_heads % self.local_kv_heads != 0 or self.num_heads % self.global_kv_heads != 0:
   295	                raise ValueError("num_heads must be divisible by both local and global KV-head counts")
   296	            if self.num_kv_heads != max(self.local_kv_heads, self.global_kv_heads):
   297	                raise ValueError("num_kv_heads must equal the stored maximum of local/global KV heads")
   298	        if self.global_every <= 0:
   299	            raise ValueError("global_every must be positive")
   300	        if self.vocab_size <= 0:
   301	            raise ValueError("vocab_size must be positive")
   302	        if self.max_seq_len <= 0:
   303	            raise ValueError("max_seq_len must be positive")
   304	        if self.num_experts <= 0:
   305	            raise ValueError("num_experts must be positive")
   306	        if self.num_experts_per_token <= 0:
   307	            raise ValueError("num_experts_per_token must be positive")
   308	        if self.num_experts_per_token >= self.num_experts:
   309	            # QB routing takes top-(k+1) and keeps the last entry as the threshold alpha, so a
   310	            # full-bank top-k asks `jax.lax.top_k` for more entries than the router has experts.
   311	            raise ValueError("num_experts_per_token must be < num_experts, because QB routing selects top-(k+1)")
   312	        if self.latent_dim is not None and not 0 < self.latent_dim <= self.hidden_dim:
   313	            raise ValueError(
   314	                f"latent_dim must be in (0, hidden_dim={self.hidden_dim}], got {self.latent_dim}; "
   315	                "it only reduces communication when it is smaller than the hidden dimension"
   316	            )
   317	        if self.shared_expert_intermediate_dim < 0:
   318	            raise ValueError("shared_expert_intermediate_dim must be non-negative")
   319	        if self.capacity_factor <= 0:
   320	            raise ValueError("capacity_factor must be positive")
   321	        if self.expert_chunks <= 0:
   322	            raise ValueError("expert_chunks must be positive")
   323	        if self.ragged_expert_chunks is not None and self.ragged_expert_chunks <= 0:
   324	            raise ValueError("ragged_expert_chunks must be positive")
   325	        if self.ragged_receiver_alignment is not None and (
   326	            self.ragged_receiver_alignment <= 0 or self.ragged_receiver_alignment % RAGGED_CUDNN_RECEIVER_ALIGNMENT != 0
   327	        ):
   328	            raise ValueError(
   329	                f"ragged_receiver_alignment must be a positive multiple of {RAGGED_CUDNN_RECEIVER_ALIGNMENT}, "
   330	                f"got {self.ragged_receiver_alignment}"
   331	            )
   332	        if self.pooled_transport_capacity_factor is not None and self.pooled_transport_capacity_factor <= 0:
   333	            raise ValueError("pooled_transport_capacity_factor must be positive")
   334	        if self.moe_implementation == "fixed_pooled_wave_all_to_all":
   335	            if self.pooled_transport_capacity_factor is None:
  1325	
  1326	
  1327	@pytest.mark.parametrize(
  1328	    "capacity,groups,arrivals",
  1329	    [
  1330	        # The hero geometry: one chunk of three experts over the production receiver capacity.
  1331	        (301466, 3, [301466, 0, 0]),
  1332	        (301466, 3, [100000, 100000, 101466]),
  1333	        (301466, 3, [99999, 100001, 65536]),
  1334	        (301466, 3, [0, 0, 0]),
  1335	        # Edges: a single group, an empty group, an exactly aligned capacity.
  1336	        (301466, 1, [301466]),
  1337	        (301466, 1, [0]),
  1338	        (1024, 4, [0, 512, 0, 512]),
  1339	        (1024, 4, [1, 1, 1, 1]),
  1340	        (768, 3, [256, 256, 256]),
  1341	    ],
  1342	)
  1343	def test_prealigned_offsets_are_a_full_aligned_partition_of_the_receiver_buffer(capacity, groups, arrivals):
  1344	    """Both halves of the kernel's contract, on the layout the transport hands it.
  1345	
  1346	    The pre-aligned path skips `pad_grouped_rows`, so nothing downstream repairs the offsets: the
  1347	    transport's buffer sizing and `full_partition_offsets` have to produce every extent a multiple
  1348	    of `_GROUP_ALIGNMENT` *and* a final offset equal to the operand's row count. Rounding the
  1349	    extents up alone gives the first and not the second.
  1350	    """
  1351	    alignment = _GROUP_ALIGNMENT
  1352	    receiver_rows = aligned_group_capacity(capacity, groups, alignment)
  1353	    assert receiver_rows % alignment == 0
  1354	    assert receiver_rows >= capacity
  1355	
  1356	    extents = np.asarray(_align_up_rows(jnp.asarray(arrivals, jnp.int32), alignment))
  1357	    assert extents.sum() <= receiver_rows, "the static buffer must cover every rounding of the routing"
  1358	
  1359	    offsets = np.asarray(full_partition_offsets(receiver_rows, jnp.asarray(extents)))
  1360	    sizes = np.diff(np.concatenate([[0], offsets]))
  1361	
  1362	    assert int(offsets[-1]) == receiver_rows
  1363	    assert all(int(size) % alignment == 0 for size in sizes)
  1364	    assert all(int(size) >= arrival for size, arrival in zip(sizes, arrivals, strict=True))
  1365	
  1366	
  1367	def test_receiver_alignment_must_satisfy_the_wgrad_kernels_group_alignment():
  1368	    """The pre-aligned path skips the kernel's own pad, so the layout has to already satisfy it."""
  1369	    assert RAGGED_CUDNN_RECEIVER_ALIGNMENT == _GROUP_ALIGNMENT == 256
  1370	    assert _resolve_receiver_alignment(None) == 1
  1371	    assert _resolve_receiver_alignment(_GROUP_ALIGNMENT) == _GROUP_ALIGNMENT
  1372	    assert _resolve_receiver_alignment(2 * _GROUP_ALIGNMENT) == 2 * _GROUP_ALIGNMENT
  1373	    # The alignments this option was written against before the kernel's real requirement was
  1374	    # known. An arm configured at one of them must not quietly run a misaligned layout.
  1375	    for stale in (1, 8, 64, 128, _GROUP_ALIGNMENT + 1, _GROUP_ALIGNMENT + 128):
  1376	        with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="multiple"):
  1377	            _resolve_receiver_alignment(stale)
  1378	    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="positive"):
  1379	        _resolve_receiver_alignment(0)
  1380	    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="positive"):
  1381	        _resolve_receiver_alignment(-256)
  1382	
  1383	
  1384	def test_receiver_alignment_routes_the_expert_mlp_to_the_prealigned_wgrad(monkeypatch):
  1385	    """An aligned receiver layout is the only thing that lets the cute path skip the pad."""
  1386	    monkeypatch.setattr(ep_ragged_all_to_all, "_quack_grouped_gemm_available", lambda: True)
  1387	    select = ep_ragged_all_to_all._select_expert_mlp
  1388	    assert select(jax.nn.silu) is ep_ragged_all_to_all._cute_expert_mlp
  1389	    assert select(jax.nn.silu, receiver_alignment=1) is ep_ragged_all_to_all._cute_expert_mlp
  1390	    assert select(jax.nn.silu, receiver_alignment=_GROUP_ALIGNMENT) is ep_ragged_all_to_all._cute_expert_mlp_prealigned
  1391	    # The portable path reads group sizes off whatever buffer it is handed, aligned or not.
  1392	    assert select(jax.nn.gelu, receiver_alignment=_GROUP_ALIGNMENT) is _ragged_dot_expert_mlp
  1393

  1395	@pytest.mark.parametrize("implementation", ["ring", "ragged_all_to_all"])

Verdict: **GO for one tightly instrumented 2-step smoke at exactly 256; NO-GO for the scored pair until the P1 evidence gaps below are closed.** I found no static offset, routing, or drop-accounting defect for the 256-row arm.

## Ranked findings

1. **P1 — “Default unset is HLO-inert” is refuted by the shared padding rewrite.**  
   [cudnn_wgrad_cute.py:151](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/cudnn_wgrad_cute.py:151) now aligns all groups and uses `full_partition_offsets`; HEAD aligned only the head groups and constructed the tail directly. They are algebraically equivalent, but lowering the exact HEAD helper and the new helper at hero geometry produced structurally different HLO: the new graph adds a three-element align/reduce/scatter path. The checked-in “inert” test merely compares implicit `None` with explicit `None` inside the new code—it never compares against HEAD ([test_moe_hero_ep.py:689](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/tests/test_moe_hero_ep.py:689)). On this CPU host it also selects the portable path, so it cannot cover the default GPU cuDNN padding path. Default numerics appear inert; byte-identical lowering is not established and the blanket SHA claim is false at helper level.

2. **P1 — The GPU correctness artifact cannot prove which cuDNN entry point ran.**  
   Both functions produced by `_build_expert_mlp_cudnn` retain the name `_expert_mlp_cudnn` ([sonic_cute.py:109](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/sonic_cute.py:109), [sonic_cute.py:139](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/sonic_cute.py:139)). The benchmark records only `selected_fn.__name__` ([ragged_ep.py:174](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/marin/src/marin/testing/moe/ragged_ep.py:174)), so control and prealigned runs report the same path. This matters because no CPU test invokes `cudnn_grouped_wgrad_prealigned`, while the internal kernel call at [cudnn_wgrad_cute.py:244](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/cudnn_wgrad_cute.py:244) enforces neither dynamic extent divisibility nor monotonic/full offsets. The prior failure was precisely a silent contract violation in this direct internal call ([incident evidence](https://github.com/marin-community/marin/issues/8339#issuecomment-5318708904)).

3. **P2 — The accepted configuration domain is broader than the byte-identity proof.**  
   `_resolve_receiver_alignment` accepts every multiple of 256 ([ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:474](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:474)), and the test explicitly endorses 512 ([test_grugformer_moe.py:1372](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/tests/grug/test_grugformer_moe.py:1372)). At hero `C=301466,g=3`, the normal 256 pad has 302,080 rows; alignment 512 produces 302,592 rows and different group starts. It remains divisible by 256 and is probably mathematically correct, but it is not the claimed byte-identical pad layout and carries unmeasured memory/FLOP cost. Either accept exactly 256 or weaken and separately validate the generalized claim.

4. **P2 — The physical-size NaN closure is correct, but its cost scales with unused capacity, not merely alignment slack.**  
   The stale-NaN hazard is real: if `cu[-1]` ends early, QuACK leaves `gu/h/dh` tail rows uninitialized, while the prealigned Wgrad folds that tail into the final group; zero in the partner operand does not neutralize NaN. Passing `physical_group_sizes` at [ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:593](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:593) safely makes all four QuACK activation-path GEMMs write the whole buffer. At balanced hero routing this means 302,080 physical rows versus about 262,144 arrivals: **15.23% more row work in each affected GEMM**. In general, work inflation is approximately `receiver_rows / arrivals`; it approaches the capacity factor under balanced routing and becomes unbounded as utilization falls. Thus the measured `+0.20%/step` is hero-specific, not a portable bound. Earlier GB200 work also found material sensitivity to capacity-filled versus active lengths ([active-length result](https://github.com/marin-community/marin/issues/8077#issuecomment-5245403247)).

## Verified concerns

- The sizing formula is safe, including empty groups, all-to-one, and nothing routed:  
  `Σ ceil(ai/A) ≤ ceil(Σai/A) + g − 1 ≤ ceil(C/A) + g − 1`.  
  At hero it is also tight; `[1, 1, C−2]` reaches the bound.
- Sender input offsets, sent sizes, return destinations, accepted rows, drop counters, and fidelity accounting remain unchanged. Only receiver starts move.
- Salted zero-init ties/sites remain distinct. The larger receiver simply enlarges the fill.
- The device transport’s update count and bytes transferred do not change, so alignment does not worsen its CTA-count limitation; only destination gaps change. The known CTA behavior is driven by update count ([incident](https://echo.oa.dev/wiki/101)).

## Required smoke evidence

Use alignment **exactly 256**, identical checkpoint/seed/batches, and require:

- The aligned 4-GPU dense-reference gate passes first, with a runtime field that uniquely records `cudnn_grouped_wgrad_prealigned`.
- Step-0 per-expert `w13` and `w2` gradient comparisons against control—not only loss or tensor-wide norms—are finite and within the established BF16 tolerance.
- Exact equality of accepted/drop counts and routing/fidelity metrics between control and treatment.
- Both updates finish with finite loss, parameters, optimizer state, `h`, and `d_gu`; no first-backward NaNs.
- HLO/XProf proves both `pad_grouped_rows` operand copies disappeared and no replacement critical-path copy appeared.
- Peak HBM remains safe and measured extra QuACK work is consistent with the 302,080/active-row ratio.

The 23 targeted CPU tests passed, but none executed the cuDNN kernel. The worktree remained unchanged.
